New Delhi , the 6th November , 1989 


S . 0 , 2948.- - . Whereas by notification of the Government of 
India in the Ministry of petroleum & Natural Gas S . U . 
No. 1554 , dated 3 - 5 - 1988 under sub -section ( 1 ) of Section 
3 of the Petroleum and Mincraly Pipelines (Acquisition of 
Right of User in Land ) Act, 1962 ( 50 of 1962 ), the Central 
Government declared its jotention of acquiro the right of 
user in lands specified in the schedule Appended to that nott 
Acation for the purpose of laying pipeline; 


और यतः सक्षम प्राधिक री ने उक्त अधिनियम की धार 6 की 
उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन सरकार को रिपोर्ट दे दी है ; 

और भागे , यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विच र करने 
के पश्चात् इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुमची में चिनिर्विष्ट भूमियों में 
उपयोग को अधिकार अजित करने का विनिरचय किया है । 

प्रम, अनः उक्त अधिनियम की धारः 8 को उपधार ( 1 ) धारा 
प्रवत्त शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्वारा घोषित करती 
है कि इस अधिसूचना में संलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट उक्त भूमियों में 
उपयोग का अधिकार पाइपलाइन विलाने के लिए एतबार । मजित किया 
जाता है । 

और मागे उस धारा को उपधार ( 4 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त पाक्तियों का 
प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार निदेश देती है कि उमरत अमियों में 
उपयोग का अधिकार केन्द्रीय सरकार में निहित होने को बज य तेल 
और प्राकृतिक गैस प्रायोग में , सभी बाधाओं से मुक्त रूप में , पोषण 
के प्रकाशन की इस तारीख को निहित होग ।

नोथ कडी सी . टी . एफ . से सरखेज तक पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए

लाइन तेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

मौर यतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतदपायद अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार प्रणित 
करना आवश्यक है । 

प्रत. प्रम पेट्रोलिगम और प्रनिज पाइप लाइन ( मि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार मा अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 
की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ने उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का प्रपना प्राशय एसद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितबर कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैम प्रायोग , निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग , मकरपुरा, गेड , बड़ौदा- 9 को 
घम अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

मौर ऐसा आक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टतः यह भी कथन 
करेगा कि क्या यह वह चाहता है कि उसकी मुमवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से 
हो या किसी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हए केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ने उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का प्रपना माशय 
एतद्द्वारा घोषित किया है ।

पायोग, मिमणि और देखभाल प्रभाग मफरपुरा रोड़, पड़ोदा - 9. को इस 
अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 


और ऐसा माक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टतः यह भी 
कयन करेगा कि क्या यह बह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यमित- गत 
पप से हो या किसीविषव्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

का . भा . 2958 - यसः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि 
लोकहित में यह पावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में इ. स . एस . 
गंधार से जी . एन . एफ . सी . सक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये 
पाइपलाइन तेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 


And , whereas, it appears that for the purpose of laying 
guch pipeline , it is necessary to acquired that right of user 
in the land described in the schedulo annexed hereto ; 


और यतः प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एरुवुपाम अनुसूली में वर्णित मृमि में उपयोग मा अधिकार मजिप्त 
फरमा मावश्यक है । 


Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub- section ( 1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine 
rals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land ) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962 ), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein : 


प्रतः मब पेट्रोलियम और पमिज पाइपलाइन ( मूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का प्रर्जन ) अधिनियम 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 
3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय 
सरकार मे उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का अपना माशय 
एतदद्वारा घोषित किया है । 


Provided that any person interested in the said land may , 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority , Oll and Natural Gas Commission , Construction 
and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodara , 
( 390 009).

पाइप लाइन बिछाने के लिए माक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस पायोग, निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड, बड़ोवा - 9. 
को इस अधिसूचना की तारीख से 11 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा ।

का . पा . 2959-- यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि 
लोकहित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में ई पी एम गंधार से 
जी एन एफ सी तक पैट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाईपलाईन तेल 
तथा प्राकृतिक गैस प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और यतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाईनों को बिछाने के 
प्रयोजन के लिये एतदुपाबस प्रमुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का 
अधिकार अजित करना मावश्यक है ।

मधिकार का अर्जन मधिनियम 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 
की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हार केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ने उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का अपना प्राशय 
एतद्द्वारा घोषित किया है । 

बणर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितब कोई व्यकिस उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के लिए पाक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस पायोग निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग मकरपुरा रोड बड़ौवा- 9 
को इस अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा ।

करेगा कि क्या यत् वह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप 
से हो या किसी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

का . मा . 296 1. - भारतीय मानक ब्यूरो नियम, 1987 के मियम 9 के उपनियम ( 1 ) के अनुसरण में भारतीय मानक ब्यूरो एतद्वारा अधिसूचित करता 
है कि जिस मानक मुहर का डिजाइन , उसके शाम्पिक विवरण पौर सम्म भारतीय मानक की संखपा वर्ष माहित नोचे अनुपूची में दो गपी है , वह निर्धारित 
कर दिया गया है । 
भारतीय मानक ग्यूरो मधिनियम , 1986 और उनके अधीन बन नियमों तथा विनियमों के प्रयोजन के लिए यह मानक मुहर 1989 - 05-01 से लागू होगी :

का . मा . 2967--- केन्द्रीय सरकार भारतीय प्रायु विज्ञान परिषद्

से परामर्श करने के पश्चात् उक्त अधिनियम की पहली अनुसूची में

का . प्रा . 2968 - - केन्द्रीय सरकार भारतीय प्रायविज्ञान परिषद 
अधिनियम , 1956 ( 1956 का 102 ) की धारा 11 की उपधारा ( 2 ) 
द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए भारतीय प्रायुविज्ञान परिषद 
से परामर्श करने के पश्चात् उक्त अधिनियम की पहली अनुसूची में

3 . Ramanandan Ram began his carrier as Pit Clerk long 
before tho nationalisation of the Coal industry . He was 
placed in Grade - II under the agreement reached between the 
management and the Indian National Trade Union Congress . 
Since tho very beginning his duties wero described in the job 
description fxed under the Coal Board Award of 1966 . In 
thç year of 1966 the management decided to standardise tho 
job of Tub Checker , Traffic Munshi and Pit Munshi and 
to designato then as Pit Clork . Under the paici schemo 
Ramanandan Ram was asked to do some additional work as 
Pit Clerk for which the management agreed to pay special 
allowance equal to two overtimo wages . Ramanandan Ram 
as well as other Pit Clerks who wore placed in Grade- II wero 
all required to do the additional work of preparing Form IV , 
issue of measurement slip and maintenance of " Lead and 
Lift" and pushing etc . for which two overtime wages were 
paid till May , 1983 when such payment of overtime wages 
was abruptly stopped . Ramanandan Ram protested against 
the stoppage of such special allowance and refused to do 
additional work . The management being enraged issucd tho 
charge- sheet on 6 -8 - 1983 against him for his wilful insubordi 
nation undor the Model Standing Orders. Ramanandan Ram 
was also placed under suspension pending the domestic en 
quiry . The management got the domestic enquiry held in 
violation of the principlo of natural justico . The Enquiry 
Officer wrongly found the concerned workman guilty of the 
charge and submitted his report. The management accepted 
the report of the Enquiry Officer and dismissed Ramanandan 
Ram from service . 


8 . As regards the Qiher concerned workman Nizamuddin

of all challans transporting coals to dcpot, as a token of

in the second shift the Dumpor Truck No , WWH 4768 un

muddin allowed the Dumper Driver to 20 away with the

said Dumper subsequently met with an accident near Nehru 
Bidyapith on Gaighata - - Ukhra roll and as & result thercof 
the Driver of the Dumper died at the spot. When the afore 
said facts came to light the management charge sheeted

and property and in accordance with the Model Standing

man from service . The management was justified in dismiss

21 . Now the question comes in whether thç managemçit 
can compel tho Pit Clerk like that CODcerned workman to 
do the job of measuring the lead and lift etc . without paying 
any extra wages by way of two overtime wages in 
a month . The facts and circumstances as revealed from 
the materials in the record and 15 discussed above , show 
that the management cannot compel the Pit Clerk like the 
concorncd workman to do such additional job without 
making payment of extra wages. There is no dispute 
to the fact that the concerned workman stopped mea 
suring the lead and lift from the date since when the manage 
ment stopped payment of extra wages by way of two 
Overtime wages in a month to the concerned workman 
for his doing the job of meaguring the lead and lift etc . 
There is no dispute to the fact that in the report dated 
1 - 8 - 1983 of the concerned workman , the concerned workman 
did not mention the measurement of the lead and lift and 
roloading for which the concerned workman had been charge 
sheeted in question is whether the concerned workman 
bas done guch act of omission in violation of the lawful 
order of the superior officer and whether such act of omis 
sion on the part of the concerned workmən constitutes in 
subordination resulting in his misconduct. 


26 . Nizamuddin in his statement before the Enquiry Officer 
has clearly stated that on 9 - 6 - 1981 he was on duty 
from 4 PM , to 12 P . M . at Haripur Siding . His statcment 
further shows that Coal was transported from K . K . Unit 
to Haripur Siding through Truck No , WMH 4768 on that 
dato . His statement further shows that hc received only 
four trips of Coal transported by the said Truck and that ho 
signed respective challans as a token of receipt of Coal. 
His statement further indicates that after unloading all the 
four trips of Coal the said Truck did not retum any more 
at the siding on that day during his period of duty . The 
management has charge -sheeted the delinquent workman for 
allowing the Dumper to go away with the loaded Coal after 
receipt of three trips of Coal. Courjously enough the 
management has not produced any challan or any document 
to show how many trips of coal wore received by Nizamu 
ddin after putting his signature on the challan . According 
to Nizamuddin s statement he received four trips of Coal 
through the Dumper No , WMH No. 4768 and signed the 
respective challans. According to the management Nizamud 
din received three trips of Coal by tho said Dumper and 
allowed the fourth trip to go away with the loaded Coal in 
connivance with the Drivor of the said Dumper with thor 
intention to sell tho Coal outside . The management has not 
produced any documentaw evidence to show that Nizamuddin 
received only three trips of Coul through the said Dumper 
on the date of occurance . The oral evidence ag adduced 
by the witnesses of the side of the management has also 
not established the involvement of the concerned workman 
as Security Guard in allowing the Dumper to go Away with 
the loaded Coal without unloading at the depot of the Com 
pany. 


22 . It has already been shown that the management cannot 
legally regulro the concerned workman to do the job of 
measuring the lead and lift etc , in addition to his normal 
duty without making the payment of extra wages. Tho 
management has not also taken resort to the benefits of pro 
visions of tho section 9 - A of the Industrial Disputes Act , 
1947 for such change in the condition of tho service of the 
Pit Clerk like the concerned workman . Be that as it may , 
the facts and circumstances established in the instant reference 
have indicated that the managementy direction upon the 
concerned workman to do the job of measuring the lead and 
lift cte, without making payment of any extra wages was not 
Tawful and that accordingly the non -compliance of such un 
lawful directions by the concerned workman does not cons 
titute the insubordination in the form of misconduct. 
The management wag therefore not justified in issuing tho 
charresheet against the concerned workman Ramanandan 
Ram for his not mentioning the micasurement of lead and 
lift in his Munshi Report dated 1 - 8 - 1983 and the Enquiry 
Officer has also not hech justified in finding the concerned 
workman gullty of the charge , Consequently the management 
has not been justified in dismissing the concerned workman 
from service . 


27. I have given due consideration to the evidence of 
A . K . Bhatt, Ex- Agent of the Colliery and the evidence of 
other witnesses on the side of the management and I find 
that the management could not establish that Nizar 
itcolved only threc trips of Coil and allowed the fourth trip 
to go away with the louted Coal in pursuaunce of any dis 
honest intention . The evidence of other witnesses like Sigir 
Ghosh , Mahendra Harija , Dhamu Mohankora do not establish 
the invortement of the del vient worknen in the alleged 
MiscCACLc . On the other hand , the evidence of Sigir Ghosh 
show s 10t alongwith the sth trip of Coal , One Ramdhan 
Saw took away some partly Alled up challang, 


23. Now I take up the case of Nizamuddin . The copy 
of the charge - shoot lovelled against him is Ext . M - 2 ( a ) . 
It appears therefrom that on 9 - 6 -1981 Nizamuddin was 
on duty in the second shift At Harinur Rollway Sinding as 
the Security Guard . Tt further annents from the charge 
sheet that on 9 -6 - 1981 in the second shift only three trips 
of coal were unloaded by Dumper No . WMH - 4768 at 
denot. It furthor appears from tho charge - shest that the 
said Dumper reached the depot at 10 . 25 P . M . with Coal 
from Kumarkhola unit and that the delinquent workman 
Nizamuddin signed one chalan as a token of Coal received 
without getting the Coal unloaded at the depot and that 
Nizamuddin allowed the Dumper Driver to come out from 
the giding through Chora Colllery Siding along with the 
loaded Dumper with an intention to sell Coal outside in 
connivance with the Driver of the Dumner . Such acts 
of the concerned workman have congtimited the misconduct 
under the Model Standing Orders according to the manage 
ment. 


24 . Undoubtedly if the alleged acts as mentioned in the 
charge - sheet are found to have been committed by the 
delinquent workman Nizamuddin , then gurely the same would 
constitute the misconduct under the Model Standing 
Ordery . The question ig whether the management succeeded 
in ostablishing the charge levelled against Nizainuddin at tho 
time of the domestic enquiry held by the Fnquiry Officer, 
The reply to tho charge given by Nizamuddin is Ext. M - 2 
( b ). In the said reply Nizamuddin denied the allegations 
made in the charge -shoct by saying that they were false and 
baseless . 


28 . (11) due consideration of the materials in the rocord I 
find that the Fnquiry Officer was not justificd in finding tho 
concerned workinen guilty of the charge specially in tho 
absence of the documentary cvidence in the form of challeng 
etc . The management could have shown by production of 
The relative register and he challuts that four thing of Coal 
were carried by the Dumpor in question and that the delin 
Cortirkninn ag Security Guri only received three tring 
of Coal hy gjening the challang and that he allowent tho 
fourth trip to go away without unloading the Coal and that 
the concerned workman sioned the challan Ag a 10 ken of 
rerelpt of Coal of the said fourth trin , It is the definito 
caso of the delinquent workman that he received four 
trips of Coal signed the respective challans, The 
management has not given any evidence also to show that 
the Coals received by the delinquont workman 
through the dumpes in question on the date 
of currence were the coals of three trips and 
not of fons tring in a case of this nature where the delin 
quent workman is charged for theft and dishoncsty in respect 
of the management s nranorty , the staprlard of nroof muat 
he satisfactory and convincing. It Apnears that the Enquiry 
Officer baging on the find of loaded dumper involved in an 
accident nens Neheru Bidyapith . came to the conclusion that 
the Joaded dumpet WAS allowed to go out ot the denot by

In the absence of material evidence to establish the involve 
ment of the delinqulient workman in such misconduct . It

4 . On an application made by the learned advocato of tho 
Company the following issue has been heard as a preliminary 
issue whether the reference is competent ? 


ADJUDICATION 


BETWEEN 


Messrs. Ashwin & Company , Arsodia 
AND 


The workmen employed under it . 


In the matter of termination of Shri M . J . Patel, Ex 

Store Keoper w . e .f . 25 - 1 - 1987 . 


APPEARANCES : 


Shri Rangwala, for the first party . 


5 . The learned advocate of the Company Shri Rangwala 
has urged before me that though in fact thc Company hag 
nove : terminated the service of Shri Patel the terms of ro 
ference clçarly indicate that there has been termination and 
Tribunal has only to decide whether the termination is legal 
or valid . In the circumstances even if the Tribunal comes to 
the conclusion that this is a clear caso of abandonment of 
the services by the concerned workman it cannot docido that 
fact. As per the terms of the reference the tribunal has to 
proceed on the basis that the services of Shri Patel have boca 
terminated and as such the reference is incompetent. For 
this proposition he has relied on a decision of Mabarashtra 
High Court, published in 1985 (I)LLJ at page 480 in the caso 
of Sitaram Vishnu Shrodkar v / s. Administrator, Govt. of 
Goa and others . In that case almost the same question had 
arisen . There also it was the case of the union that tho 
management had terminated the services of the workmen , 
while it was the case of the managernent that the workman , 
had absen ed from duty and abandoned the job . The Gov

Shri Memon , for the second party . 


AWARD 


The Govt. of India, Ministry of Labour by its Notifica 
tion No . L -29012 /20 /88-D . III( B ) dt. 26 -7-88 has referred an 
Industrial dispute noted below , between M / s . Ashwin & Com 
pany , Arsodia (hereinafter referred to as Company and its 
workmen , to the Tribunal at Ahmedabad under the provi 
słons of Section 10 ( 1) (d ) and sub - section 2 (a ) of Section 10 
for adjudication . It has been ultimately referred to me. As per 
the schedule , following dispute has to be adjudicated . Whether 
the action of the management of M / s . Ashwin & Company, 
Arsodla , in terminating the services of Shri M . J, Patel, Ex | 
Store -keeper with effect from 25 - 1 - 1987 is legal and justified ? 
If not, what relief the workman is entitled to . 


" The Tribunal could not travel beyond the reference and 

decide the question whether the respondent No. 4 
Jiad abandoned his sorvices. That the petitioner had 
terminated the services of the respondent No . 4 was 
an act fastened on the petitioner by this reference 
and the only question loft open for decision was 
whether the termination was legal and propor . În 
this view of the matter, in our opinion , tho retorence 
itself was bad and has to be quashed ." 


2 . The concerned workman has filed his statement of 
claim at Ex. 6 . According to him he was working as a 
store-keeper at the mincs of the Company at Arsodia from 
1 -8 - 1964. He was paid a total monthly salary of Rs. 400 p . m . 
but was not given any benefit of leave . The salary paid to 
him wiis less than the minimum wages payablo under the 
Minimum Wages Act, and he had therefore demanded leave 
benefits and pay . He was therefore, dismissed from servico 
from 25 -1 - 1987 . He approached tho authorities but in vain . He 
therefore made an application to the Conciilation Officer but 
thero also the Company refused to reinstate him and made 
false allegations against him of mismanagement and mis 
appropriation 


The Bombay High Court very strongly relied for the abovo 
vicw on the decision of the full bench of the Delhi High 
Court in M /s. India Tourism Dev, Corporation , New Delhi, 
v / s. Delhi Administration and Others (1982 - Lab IC p. 1309 ). 
Before the Delhi High Court also almost same type of 
question had arisen , There, the dispute was whether there 
was lock out or closure and the reference was termed as 
follows: 


" Whether the workman as shown in Annexure A aro 

cotiled to wages for a period of lockout with effect 
from 1st January , 1981 and if so , what directions 
are necessary in this respect ?" 


The Delhi High Court in that case observed as follows :

का . प्रा . 2987. -- केन्द्रीय सरकार, कर्मचारी राज्य बीमा अधिनियम , 1918 ( 1948 का 34 ) की धारा 91क के साप पठित धारा 88 नारा 
प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए और भारत सरकार श्रम मंत्रालय की नारीब 8 सितम्बर , 37 की अधिसूचना ममा 2557 के क्रम में उक्त अधिनियम 
प्रवर्तन से मैसर्स भारत पेट्रोलियम कारपोरेशन लिमिटेड में नियुक्त नियमित कर्मचारियों को प्रथम अक्तूबर , 87 मे 30 सितम्बर , 91 तक की जिसमें यह विनाक 
भी सम्मिलित है फी, पीर अवधि के लिए छूट प्रदान करती है । 

1. पूर्वोक्त छूट की शर्ते निम्नलिखित है, अर्थात् :--- 
( 1) पूर्वोक्त कारस्थाना , जिसमें कर्मचारी नियोजित है, एका रजिस्टर रम्नेगा , जिसमें छूट प्राप्त कर्मचारियों के नाम और पदामिधान दिखाए आएंगे, 
( 2) इस छूट के होते हुए भी, कर्मचारी उक्त अधिनियम के अधीन ऐमी प्रसुविधाएं प्राप्त करते रहेंगे , जिनको पाने के लिए वे इम मधिसूचना द्वारा 

प्रवृत्त होने की सारीख से पूर्व संबन अभिदायों के थाधार पर हकदार हो जाते, 
( 3 ) छूट प्राप्त अवधि के लिए यदि कोई अभिवाय पहले ही किए जा चुके हों तो पे वापस नहीं किए जाएंगे , 
( 4 ) उक्त कारखाने का मियोजक , उस अवधि की घायत जिसके दौराम उस कारखाने पर उक्त अधिनियम प्रवर्तमाम था (जिसे इसमें इसके पश्चात 
_ " उक्त प्रवधि " कहा गया है ) , ऐसी वियणियां मे प्रारूप में और ऐसी विशिष्टियों सहित देगा को कर्मचारी राज्य बीमा ( साधारण ) बिनियम , 

1950 के प्रधान उक्त अवधि को बामन वेती थी , 
( 5 ) निगम द्वारा उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 45 की उप -धारा ( 1 ) के अधीन नियुक्त किया गया कोई निरीक्षक, पा निगम का इस निमिस प्राधिकरा 

कोई अन्य पवधारी : - - 
(i ) धारा 44 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन , उक्त अवधि को बाबत वी गई किमी विवरणी की विशिष्टियों को सत्यापित करने के प्रयोजनार्ष , 
(ii ) यह अभिनिश्चित करने के प्रयोजनार्थ कि कर्मचारी राज्य बीमा (माधारण ) विनियम , 1950 द्वारा प्रथा अपेक्षित रजिस्टर मौर, अभिलेख 

उक्त अवधि के लिए रखे गये थे या नहीं : मा 
( iii ) यह अमिनिश्चित करने के प्रयोजनाप कि कर्मचारी , नियोजक द्वारा दिये गए जन फायदों को , जिसके प्रतिफल स्वरूप इस अधिसूचना के 

प्रधीन छूट दी जा रही है, नकद में प्रार यस्तु रूप में पाने का हकदार बना हुआ है या नहीं, या 
(iv ) यह अभिनिश्चित करने के प्रयोजनार्थ कि उस अवधि के दौरान, जब उक्त कारखाने के संबंध में अधिनियम के उपबम्म प्रपत्त थे , ऐसे किन्हीं

दस्तावेज, ऐसे निरीक्षक या अन्य पदधारी के समक्ष प्रस्तुत करें और उनकी परीक्षा करने थे, या उन्हें ऐसी जानकारी , जिसे वे प्रावश्यक

3 . पागे म्पन किया कि प्रार्थी श्रमिक ने उसको बीमार होने की 
सूचना ममय समय पर वी और उसने चिकित्मीय प्रमाण पत्र पर्व मेडीकल 
फिटनेम प्रमाण पत्र भी श्री व्यास को भिजवा दियं । प्रागे व्यक्त किया कि 
प्रार्थी श्रमिक को दिनांक 3-10- 86 के प्रदेश द्वार प्रांच मैनेजर स्टेट

उपस्थित हो जाये ऐसा न करने पर यह मान नेना निखा कि वह नोटिम 
अवधि समाप्ति के पश्चात् सेवा का म्येच्छा में परित्याग हो चुका है । प्रागे 
यक्त किया कि प्रार्थी श्रमिक की सेवाएं समाप्त करने का कोई प्रादेश 
आरी नहीं किया गया और न ही शमा प्रबन्धक स्टेट बैफ माफ इण्डिया 
रीगम प्राी श्रमिक की सेवा समाप्त करने के लिए मक्षम था प्रत्यर्थी 
प्रबन्धक ने प्रार्थी श्रमिक की सेवाएं ममाम्त करने का कोई मादेश नाही 
निकाला । मागे यह भी व्यक्त कियाफ प्रार्थी श्रमिक को मेवाएं ममाप्त 
फरने में पूर्व उसको प्रागेप पन दिया जाना व बचाव का प्रथमर 
दिया जाना प्रावश्यकः । । मगर प्रार्थी श्रमिक को कोई नियमानुसार 
कोई प्रारोप पत्र नहीं दिया गया और न ही सुनवाई का अवसर दिया । 
मागे यह भी व्यक्त किया कि प्रार्थी भिक को धारा 25एफ अधिनियम 
का कोई नोटिम भी नहीं दिया । अंतिम प्रार्थना की कि प्रार्थी श्रमिक को 
समस्त लाभ सहित स्टेट बैंमः आफ इण्डिया में पुनः सेवा में लिया जाये

डाक जारी किये गये । अप्रार्थी नियोजक की पोर मे प्रारम्भ में श्री

हो गया और ऐमा हो नादिम प्रबन्धक , हैवी वाटर प्रोजैक्ट के . रावत 
भाटा को भी प्राप्त हो गया । इन नोटिसम की प्राप्ति के बाद नियोजक

AWARD 
By exercising its powers under Section 1011)(d ) and Suli

mentioned in the order of his initial appointment, yet he 
was infact appointed on probation in terms of the atoresor

the workman was on probation right from the date of his

Academy Contributory Provident Fund and according to 
Sahitya Academy Contributory Provident Fund Rules 1954 
this facility of contribution to the provident fund is not 
available to temporary employees other than those appoint 
ed on probation . It is the further case of the workman 
that he had served the Sahitya A ademy for continuously 
for a period of 2 - 1 / 2 years and his termination amounted

was given to liini nor any retrenchment conpenght on was 
paid to him and hence the order of termination is in viola

workman had raised certain other ground , su h 80 order 
of terniination is punitive in character and as no charge 
sheet had been servod on him and ho had not been given 
any opportunity to defend himself , it was in violation of 
the principles of natural justice and that the order of termi 
nation was passed by authority lower in rank and status 
to his appointing authority and in this way the order of 
termination was bad in law . However , these grounds were 
not pressed at the time of arguments ,

4 . The contention of the Management is that the Sahitya 
Academy is not an Industry and that the Ld . Govt. Delhi 
Administration is the appropriate Government and Central 
Government is not Appropriate Government in this case 
and for this reason the reference is bad . It may be men 
tioned here that the question whether the reference is bed 
on the grounds mentioned was trented as preliminary and 
it was decided vide Order of this Tribunal dated 28 - 1 - 1988 
and it was held that both the Central Government and the 
Lt. Governor had the power to make reference of the In 
dustrial Disputes in the Union Territory of Delhi and in 
the present dispute the Central Government was the appro 
priate Government and the reference was valid and the 
Tribunal had jurisdiction . As regards the objection that 
the Sahitya Academy is not an Industry it was considered 
bw the ALC ( C ) Delhi and he took thc view that is per 
the interpretation of the Hon ble Supreme Court, the Sahitya 
Academy is covered within the purview of Section 20 ) of 
the I, D , Act . As the Academy is a literasy institution and 
engages itself in the activity of publishing and selling books 
relating to Indian Literature and this activity of publication 
and sale satisfies the human wants and requirements . I am 
inclined to agree with the view taken by the A . L . C . (Delhi) 
and hold that the activities and functions of the Sahitya 
Academy meet the triple test of Industry laid down in the 
atuhority Bangalore Water Supply and Sewerage Board Vergue 
A . Rajappa and others AIR 1978 Supreme Court 549 . Henco 
it is " industry " within the meaning of section 20 ) of the 
I . D . Act .

6 . Whether the workman wag on probation or in tempo 
rary employment would really not make much differenco 
because the Management has admittedly not complied with 
the provisions of Section 25F of the I. D . Act. It is not dis 
puted that the workman had put in 2 - 1 / 2 years of continuous 
service with the Management and therefore he had sailed into 
the Protection of section 25 - F of the ID . Act. Even a pro 
hationer who completes one vears continuous service as de 
fined under section 25 - B of the I. D . Act is covered by the 
provisions of section 25 -F of the ID Act. In the authority 
cited 29 Managoment of Karnataka State Road Transport 
Corporation Bangalore Versus M . Borainh and Another and 
Karnataka State Road Transport Corporation , Bangalore 
Versus Sheikh Abdul Khader and Others 1984 ( 1 ) SCC 244 it 
was held as under : 
wabour and Services Industrial Disputes Act 1947. Soc . 

tions 2 (oo ) and 25 - Termination of services of pro 
bationer for unsuitability amounts to retrenchment 
Non - compliance with Section 25 - F in case of such 
termination - Effect . 
Held 

Section 2 (00 ) covers every case of termination of 
service except those which have been cmbodied in 
the definition . Therefore , discharge from employ 
ment or termination of service of a probationcr , would 
also amount to retrenchment. Compliance with tho 
requirements of Section 25- F in case of such termi 
nation is essential. In the present cases however, Scc 
tion 25 - F was not complied with nor was it disputed 
hy the employer -appellant that as a necessary con 
seruence of non - compliance with Section 25- F in a 
cace where it annlled the order of terminion wag 
rendered void . The High Court wag , therefore . 
justified in concluding that the order of retrenchment 
was had . ”

Įise the fee /staff structure by re - grouping the categories per

of all India Radio . The worknian has contended that since

of Director General, All India Radio and thus enjoyed the

rationalisation / upward revision to bring their scales also in

loyees in A . I. R . as done in the case of Staff Artists on sound 
broadcasting side . This was all the more essential in view 
of the fact that there were certain categories in Telvision 
viz . Musicians, script writers , Assistant Producers, Producers 
etc ., which were common with A . I . R . and if their scales were 
not rationalised then at the time of granting Pay Commis . 
sion benefits they would get lower revised fee scales resulting 
in internal differentials in the matter of revised fee scales 
as well as suffer apparent anamolies in the revised fee scales 
w . e . f, 1 - 1 - 73 . Thus in the interest of equality and parity 
with their counterparts in A . I . R . , the aims and objectives of 
Rationalisation should have been uniformly implemented , 
More so , because Television is admittedly much more com 
plex , arduous and complicated requiring much more effort and 
specialisation than sound broadcasting , the fee scales in TV 
could not possibly be lower than the scales on sound broad 
casting side. These should at least be the same as in A . I. R . 

The Management has admitted the contentions of the work . 
man with regard to the aims and objectives of rationalisa 
tion viz . to reduce multicplicity and to bring their scales in 
line with the standard scales of regular government servants . 
As per management s own case , the purpose was essentially 
rationalisation and upward revision in the process was only 
incidental. The Management has, however , contended that 

rationalisation on Television side was not considered 
necessary at that time because TV was still in the developing 
stage when there was only one TV Centre in the country . 
On this logic the management has contended in its written 
statement that the Ministry s order dated 7 - 2 - 72 was not 
applicable to Staff Artists on TV side of A . I. R . This con 
tention of the Management is contradicted by the Manage 
ment s own witness in his Cross -examination dated 24 - 3 -87

the scales of TV Artists have also been rationalised .

ISSUE NO . 6 : 


As per the roder of the Ministry of Finance dated 
10 - 1 - 1977 ( Ex . W .42 ) it was laid down that those Employees 
aie entitled to tie grant of Selection grade ( 1 ) where the 
pots are filled by direct recruitment to an extent of not less 
than 70 per cent and ( 2 ) where the employees are suffering 
accute Stagnation . There was also another order of the 
Ministry of Finance dated 24 - 10 -80 whereby para lív ) of 
the Order dated 10 - 1 - 1977 ( W .42 ) was amended to relax 
the criterion about the length of service for determining 
eligibility oi the Selection Grade . In the case of the work 
man , however, the criteria of length of service is immaterial 
rainer the criteria of accute stagnation is relevant and an 
important factor. 


On the principles and guidelines of the above mentioned 
Order of the Ministry of Finance dated 10 - 1 - 1977 ( EX , W . 42 ) 
the Presiuent was pleased to sanction Selection Grades for 
9 categories of Staff Artists of Doordarshan vide Ministry s 
order dated 11- 11 - 1977 ( Ex . W .41 ) . This order was made 
applicable subject to the fulfilment of various conditions 
laid down in the Memorandum of Ministry of Finance 
( Ex . W .42 ) and especially subject to paras 1 ( i) , ( v ) , ( vii ) 
and para 4 of the Ministry of Finance order dated 10 - 1 - 1977 
(Ex. W .42 ) . The workman contends that even when his 
case was covered , he was deprived and discriminated against 
in the grant of Selection Grade.

i 


. The case of the workman is that he fulfilled both the 

laid down criteria for the grant of selection grade as he 
was directly recruited as a Makeup Artist on 1 - 9 - 1961 as 
well as he suffered an accute stagnation in the pre -revised 
fee scale of Rs. 300 -25-6001 after 1 - 1 - 1973 . The workinan

The Management has now annexed a copy of their Menlo 
randuin dated 20 - 4 - 83 based on the Ministry s order dt . 
(4 - 2 -77 , stating that the contract of the workman is extend 
e : upto the age of 58 years . Thus the employer has clearly

criginal contract of the workman dated 1 - 1 - 62 to follow the 
Jules & practice in vuge in AIR TV . The issue No. 6 is thus 
Cisposted of with these directions to the Management. 


The Management has defended the case by referring 
the Recruitment Rules of 1979 and contended that the 
vacancies in the category of Makeup Artist were to be 
filled 100 per cent by promotion , The Management is , 
however , silent about the factor of accute stagnation in the 
guidelines . 


The contentions of the Management are untenable in 
law because the Ministry s order dated 11- 11 - 77 was issued 
much before the Recruitment Rules of 1979 came into 
existence . Alsu , since the Recruitment Rules are not notifi 
ed they do not have the fore of law . It is also relevant 
to mention here that on the material date the concerned 
workman was not granted even the revised ree scale on 
the analogy of lllrd Pay Commission w .e . f . 1 - 1 - 1973. 


1 


However, a proposal was initiated by the Management 
of Doordarshan in 1979 for the sanction of Selection Grade 
as per the provisions of Ministry of Finance order dated 
10 - 1 - 1977 ( Ex . 42 ) for Makeup Artists in TV , admitting 
therein that the Makeup Artists, including the workman , are 
entitled to the grant of Selection Grade w .e . 1 , 1 - 8 - 1976 on 
the laid down criterion of acute stagnation . The proposal 
of the Management was wrongly rejeced by the Ministry 
of I & B because it was incorrectly shown in the Manage 
ment s proposal that the workman was not stagnation at 
the maximum of the pre - revised scale of Rs. 300 -25 -600 in 
1973. The proposal & decision is at Ex. W . 43 . It is a 
matter of fact that the workman had reached the maximum 
of the scale i. e . Rs. 600 on 1 - 1 - 1973 and thereafter no 
increment was allowed to him and since that date ho 
suffered an accute stagnation . Thus, the workman has a 
prima facie case for getting Selection Grade . In the pecu 
liarity of circumstances as above , and in view of the guide 
lines laid down in the Ministry of Finance order dated 
10 - 1 - 77 I hold that the workman is entitled to the Selection 
Grade and consequential benefits w . e . f. 1 - 8 - 1976 .

hereby publishes the award of the Central Government 
Industiral Tribunal (No . 2 ) , Dhanbad as shown in the Anne 
xure in the industrial dispute between the employers in relat on 
to the management of Koordih Colliery of M / s . Bharat Cok 
ing Coal Ltd . and their workmen , which was received by 
the Central Government on 28 -10 - 1989 . 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 

TRIBUNAL (NO . 2 ) AT DHANBAD 


Short recital of the Case 
Shri B . K . Sinha , the workman concerned submitted his 
joining report on transfer dated 17 - 10 -85 to G . M . Block - II 
and thereafter he absented from duty continuously without 
permission and without satisfactory cause . After waiting for 
nearly two months he was issued charge sheet . It was addres 
sed to his local address and also to his permanent address 
at Patna . The registered letter returned by the Postal Depart 
ment undelivered as the addressee could not be treated . Copy 
of the letter intending for local address was given to Shri 
S . k . Lall, S . I. Security , but he reported that on 2 occasions 
he tried to contact Shri Sinha on his local add : css , but could 
not find him there returned the same undeliicred . 


On the basis of the enquiry held , the Enquiry Officer 
gave his finding to the effect that the charges framed against 
Shri B . A . Sinha was fully established beyoni doubt Consi 
dering the gravity of the misconduct against the workman 
Management came to the controsion that this is a fit case 
for dismissal from service . Accordingly the workman con 
cerned was dismissed from service 


Reference No. 132 of 1982 
In the matter of an indusutrial dispute under Section 10 ( 1 ) (d ) 

of the J . D . Act , 1947 
PARTIES : 
Employers in relation to the management of Koordih 

Colliery of Messrs Bharat Coking Coal Limited , 
· Post Office Sonardih , Distt . Dhanbad and their

Dhanbad , the 16th October, 1989 

AWARD) 
The Government of India , Ministry of Labour and Reha 
bilitation in exercise of the powers conferred on them under 
Section 10 ( 1 ) ( d ) of the I . D . Act, 1947 has referred the 
following dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication vide their 
Order No . L -20012 ( 197) / 82 -DJII ( A ) dated 5 - 11- 1982 . 

SCHEDULE 
“ Whether the demand of the workmen of Koordih 

Colliery of Messrs Bharat Coking Coal Lisnited , 
Post Office Sonardih , District Dhanbad for protec 
tion of wages and proper categorisation in resnect 
of S Shri Bahadur Singh , Abedin Mian , Kamruddin 
Mian , Munshi Mahato , Rameshwar Das and Ramu 
Uria is justified ? If so , to what relief are the 
workman concerned entitled ?": 


Union s contention is that the dismissal is illegal and un 
justified . The workman use i to woik as Security Sub 
Inspecior in Block - II , which is no less than 25 Kms away 
from Sijua , his official residence where he used to perform 
1 ; is dutie : every day. Due 1c long travel and shift duty 
the Wirkman was gradually deteriorating in health . The 
Volkman participated in enquiry and gave the document of 
his ilinss , but dismissal is disproportionate to the alleged 
misconduct. After all dismissal is a capital punishment and 
infliction of said punishment was uncalled for. 

After prolonged dicussions on several dates it was decided 
to settle the case on the following terms and conditions : 

Terms of Settlement 
( 1 ) Shri B . K . Sinha will be taken into employment 

with immediate effect without back wages. 
(2 ) The period of idleness from 17 - 10 -85 till he joins 

will be treated as leave without pay. 
(3 ) Shri Sinha will have no claim over the back wages 

during the period he was idle. 
(4 ) Copy of this settlement will be submitted to the 

Hon ble Tribunal No. 1 for passing the award in 

terms of this settlement. 
Union s Representative : 

(G . D . PANDEY ) 

(B . K . SINHA ) . 
Witness : 

Illegible 
Management Representative : 

( A . A . JAFRI) 
( B . P . YADAV ) : 


In this case both the parties appeared and filed their res 
pective W .S . documents etc. Thereafter the case proceeded 
along its course . Subsequently at the stage of oral evidence 
both the parties appeared before me and filed a petition of 
compromise . I heard both the parties on the said petition 
of compromise and I do find that the terms of compromise 
are fair, proper and beneficial to both the parties. Accord 
ingly I do accept the said petition of compromise and pass 
an Award in terms of the compromise petition which forms 
part of the Award as Annexure . 

I. N . SINHA , Presiding Officer

2 . The case of the management of Kedla Opencase Project 
of Ms. Central Coalfields Ltd ., Kedla , Hazaribagh , as 
appearing from the written statement filed , details apart, 
is as follows : 


4 . The management has examined one witness namely 
MW - 1 Sri C . Ram , earlier posted in Personnel and AU 
liinistrative Department of Central Coalfields Ltd .. and 
laid in evidence a mass of docunents which have been inark 
ed as Exts . M - 1 to M - 10 / 1. On the other hand , the spon 
soring union was not examined any witness includ 
ing ine concerned workman , but introduced in evidence 
one item of document an Ottice Order dated 1 - 9 - 75 marked 
as Ext . W - 1 . 


5 . In conformity with the case of the management Sri 
. C . Ram who was posted firstly as Administrative Officer 
and later as Sr. Fersonnel Officer ( Administration ) at Kedla 
Upencast Project between the period from 1980 to 1987 
has stated that Kedla Opencase Project is a nationalised 
colliery and that the project falls within the jurisdiction 
of Hazaribagh Area of Central Coalfields Ltd . He has 
further stated taat Kedla Opencase Project includes Kedla 
Opencase mine , Kedla North colliery and Keila South 
Coiliery and that the Project Officer is the head of the Prü 
ject. According to his testimony Kedla Opencaes Project 
is considered as one integrated unit for the purpose of con 
sideration of promotion from the post of Lower Division 
Clerkt Upper Division Clerk . 


The reference is bad in law and it is not maintainable 
because the promotion is the sole function of the manage 
.ment and no employee can claim it as of right . The sub 
stantive case of the management is that the Central Coalfields 
Limited has divided its collieries into various areas for the 
purpose of administrative convenience and more effective 
administration . Each Area consists of a group of co 
guous collieries . The Areas were previously headed by 
Area General Managers who were later re - designated as 
General Managers . Hazaribagh Area is one of such area 
and includes a number of collieries of which Kedla Open 
cast Project is one. This Kedla Opencase Project consists 
of kedla Opencast mine , Kedla North Colliery & Kodla 
South Colliery . All the collieries placed under a Project 
Officer is treated as one unit i.e . the Kedla Opencast Pro 
ject. According to the rules of the management and the 
actual practice Clerks Grade- II (i.e . Lower Division Clerks ) 
are considered for selectior for promotion to the post of 
Clerks Grade I ( the Upper Division Clerk ) Project Unit -wise 
ori the basis of seniority and suitability . In this case Kedla . 
Opencast Project consisting of Kedla Opencast mine , Kedla 
North Mine and Kedla South mine are one unit since they 
are undiche Project Officer . The concerned workman 
was appuirited as Clerk Glade - II with effeci from 1 - 10 - 73 . 
He held such a post and discharged his duties of Clerk 
Grade- II til ! 31 - 3 - 81. He was thereafter promoted to ihe 
post of Lista Grade- I with cifect from 31- 3- 31 . During 
this entire reriod and even till 5 - 9 - 83 he did not discharge 
the duties of any higher post. He was transferred from 
Kedla North Colliery to the Office of the General Manager, 
Hazaribagh Area at Charhi in his existing capacity as Clerk 
Grade- II by Office Order dated 8 - 2 - 80 . While he was work 
ing as Clerk Grade - II in the Office of the General Manager , 
Hazaribagh Area some of his juniors working as Clerks 
Grade - II in Kedla Opencast Project were selected and pro 
moted to the post of Clerk Grade- I by a Departmental 
Promotion Committee with effect from 31- 3 -81 . The Office 
of the General Manager, Hazaribagh is a separaie unit for 
the purpose of consideration of Clerk Grade- II for selec 
tion to the post of Clerk Grade - 1 . The concerned workman 
represented his case through his union , Rashtriya Colliery 
Mazdoor Sangh by their letter dated 15 - 7 -81 staung that 
he should be transferred back to Kedla North Collierny SO 
that he might be able to get his promotion . It was also 
stated in the same letter that there was no possibility of 
his getting a chance for promotion in the Office of the 
General Manager ; Hazaribagh which was a separate unit 
for the purpose of consideration of Clerk Grade- Il for 
selection to the post of Clerk Grade -I . Accordingly the 
management by Office Order dated 24 27 -7 -81 transferred 
the concerned workman back to Kedla North Colliery with 
immediate effect . He joined his duties there . His case 
thereafter was considered and he was given promotion to 
the post of Clerk Grade I with retrospective effect from 
31- 3 -81 by an Office Orded issued on 5 - 9 -83 i.e . the date 
from which his juniors in Kedla Opencast Project were 
promoted . He was also paid the necessary arrears . It is 
eviddent that he was given promotion when due for the 
first time ond there is , therefore , no justification whatsoever 
for the present reference . In the circumstances the manage 
ment has . prayed that the present reference be disposed of 
as it has berome redudant and superfluous . 


6 . It is the irrefragable position that the concerned work 
man joined the service of Kedla Opencase Project on 
1 - 10 - 73 as Clerk Grade - Il wilich is comparable to the post 
of Lower Division Clerk . He continued to work as such 
when he was transferred from Kedla North Colliery 20 
the Office of the General Manager , Hazaribagh Area by 
Office Order dated 8 - 2 - 80 . The management has produced 
the Office Order dated 8 - 2 -80 whereby the concerned work 
man was transferred from Kedla North Colliery to the 
Office of the General Manager ( H ) Ext. M - 2 . It appears 
that while he was posted as Clerk Grade- II in the Officer 
of the General Manager at Hazaribagh some of his juniors 
working as Clerk Grade - II in Kedla Opencase Pdoject were 
selected and promoted to the post of Clerk Grade - [ by 
Department Promotion Committee with effect from 31 - 3 - 81 . 
The Office Order with regard to the promotion of these 
workmen dated 30131- 3 - 81 has been narked as Ext. M - 1 . 
Thereafter the concerned workman represented through 
his union , Rasatriya Colliery Mazdoor Sangh by leiter dated 
15 - 7 -81 that he should be transferred back to Kedla North 
Colliery so that he could get promotion . The representation 
of the sponsoring union dated 15 - 7 - 81 has been marked as 
Ext. M - 3 . It appears that the management, in response 
to the representation of the union , transferred him back to 
Kedla North Colliery by Office Order dated 24 :27 - 7 -81 
(Ext . M - 4 ) . It further appears that the management con 
sidered the case of the concerned workman for his promo 
tion to the post of Clerk Grade - { and actually promoted 
him to the post of Clerk Grade - 1. This is evident by the 
letter of the Personnel Manager ( H ) Charhi to the Project 
Officer , Kedla dated 24 - 8 - 83 ( Ext . M - 5 ) and the Office 
Order dated 5 - 9 -R3 promoting the concerned workman to 
the post of Clerk Grade - I with effect from 31 - 3 - 81 (Ext . 
M - 6 ) . It appears from the order of promotion that ihe

31- 3- 81 Ext. M -6 .

WA. II and

Union . The management of the colliery iy very much biased 
and prcjudiced against the members of Bihar Coll ery Kam 
gar Union . It is the statutory and moral obligation of the 
managcment to supply water to its employcon. The job of

foly - wages is legut und justified . In the circumstances , the 
sponsoring union has prayed that the present reference be 
answered in favour of the workman ,

AWARD 
The Central Government, in cxercise of the powers confer 
red by clause (d ) of sub - section ( 1 ) and sub - section 2 ( A ) of 
Section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 has referred 
the following dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication : 
“ Whether the action of the General Manager , The Sangli 

Bank Limited Sangli in revertitig Shri Sham L . 
Chavan , a confirmed clerk in the bank to the post 
of sub - staff w . e . f. 3 - 7 - 86 is legal and justificd ? If 

not, to what relief the workman is entitled to ? " . 
2 . The workmen represented by the General Secretary , 
Sangli Bank Employees Union filed their statement of claim 
and the Employer , the Sangli Bank Ltd . filed written state 
ment contesting the claim . However , today the parties field 
a joint application / pursis stating that the partics have como 
to an amicable settlement ind praying for an award in terms 
of the settlement arrived at 
3 . The terms of the settlement are as follows : 
" 1. Both the particg agreed that whatever stands an 

counters taken by both the parties against cach 

other stand As withdrawn in view of the settlement. 
2 . Shri Sham Laxman Chavan who was reverted to sub 

staff cadre from clerical cadre from 3rd July 1986 
and was posted as Care Taker at Head Office , 
shall now be deemed to bo posted in clerical cadro 
w . . .f . 3rd July 1986 and all consequential bencfits 
such as pay scale etc , shall be given and paid to 
him . The management agrecs to pay the artears , 
subject to normal deductions , within one month 

from this date , 
3 . The Union and tlię workman concerned agree that 

they shall not reopen the case of Shri Sham Laxman 

Chavan at any forum hereafter on any count, 
4 . Both the partics would hear their own respective 

costs, " 
4 . The settlement is ſair and is in the best interest of 
liarmonious industrial relations in general and both the parties 
in particular. I thereforç pass an award in the above quoted 
terms of the settlement, with a direction that tho management 
to pay to the concerned workman all the consequential arrears 
within one month from today . 

M , S . JAMDAR , Presiding Officer

AWARD 


3 . A . regards the rule of recruitment of the relatives 

of the employees of the Bank are concorosd , It is 
agreed that the Bank shall consider employing 
close relatives of the employees who have displayed 
Integrity beyond doubt, who possess all the essen 
tial roquirements , qualifictions etc . And if such re 
lative k otherwira eleible for employmont on merits 
and closeness of relationship . Howover, it is 
agreed that such facility of employment of a re 
lative of an employee shall only be allowed and 
extended to one close relative ( Son , Daughter , 
Spouso . Brother and Sister ) and that only subject 
to availability of an existing vacancy .

12 . Tho workman is defined in 
Act as follows : - - 


Fi Sankaran knowledging 


" Workman means any person ( including an apprentico) 

employed in any industry to do any manual, 90 
skilled , skilled , technical, Operational, clorical or 
supervisory work for hire or reward , whothor the 
terms of employment be express or implied . . . " 


From this definition it can be seen that in order to bo a 
workman the following conditions aro to be satisfied - - 

(a) He should be employed in an industry, 
( b ) He should be employed to do any manual, ungkilled , 

skilled , technical, operational, clerical or suporvisory 

work , 
(c) IIc should be employed for hire or l eward , and 
( d ) There must be terms of employment. The terms can 

be uxpress or implied ,

A Money Dce is not ompluyod to do manual, unskilled , skill 
ed , technical, operational, clerical or supervisory work. More 
over there is no contract or employment either express or 
implied , Hero the contract is that of agency and not that 
of employłnent. So also the " industrial dispute " neans, any 
dispute or difference between omployers and employcos, or 
betweon employers and workmen , or between workmon and 
workmen , which is connected with the employment or non 
omployment or the terms of employment or with the condi 
tions of labour or any person . So the subject matter of tho 
lispute must be connected with the employment or non 
employment of the terms of employment or with the condi 
tions of labour. In this case there is no relationship of emp 
loyer and workman between the Management and Monoy 
, Beu . ] t can be seen from tho testimony of WW1 that his 
job was canvassing business for the Bank , Canvassing of 
business is not a work in the nature of manual, unskilled , 
skillect, technical, operational, clerical or supervisory . In tho 
cross -examination he would deposo that in order to appoint 
a person as Money Boo there is no prescribed academic 
qualification und anybody having knowledge in reading and 
writing and some accounting can be appointed as Money 
Bce. There is no prescribed age limit . There is no retirement 
age and there is no selection process. The Money Boo will get 
commission on the basis of tho amount advanced by them ,

appointed and authorised Money Bee Shri Appu died on

Sbți Appu in collecting the amount under tho door to door 
collection scheme. Therefore I find that the workman Shri 
M . P . Sankarankutty is not entitled to any relief in this rofer 
ence , An award is passed accordingly .

14 . The learned counsel for the workman would argue 
relying on the decision reported in AIR 1984 S . C . 1462 that 
the words anly skilled or upskilled manual, supervisory , lech 
nical or clerical work are not intended to limit or narrow 
the amplitudo of the definition of "workmat on tho other 
hand they indicato and emphasise tho broad swoop of the 
definition which is designed to cover all mannor of posson : 
employed in an industry , irrespectivo of whother they aro 
engaged in killed work or unskilled work , manual work , 
gupervisory work , technical work or clorical work and obvi 
ously the broad intention is to tako i tho entire labour 
force and exclude the managerial force . That, of course , is 
As it should be. The learned counsel further argued that 
the Development Officers of tho Life Insuranco Corporation 
have been considered to be workmon as defined in tho Act 
as they have no supervisory powers and they have no gub 
ordinate staff and their actions are controlled and Tobulatod 
by the Life Insurance Corporation of India .

hird of Ioward 
( ii ) He should be engaged in skilled or unskilled manual.

(iii ) He should not be a person falling under any of 

the exompted four clauses, i.e ., 1 to 4 montioned 
under the definition of workman in Soç , 20) of tho

8. My findings on the additional issues and the point of 
reference are as follows : 

ISSUE NUMBER 1 

Yes 
ISSUE NUMBER 2 

No 
· ISSUE NUMBER 3 


Yes 


POINT OF REFERENCE 


The action of the management of Tungabhadra Grameen 
Bank , H . O . Bellary in terminating the services of Shri Ishwar 
D . Audi, Probationary Field Supervisor, with effect from 
31. 1 - 1987 is justified . He is not entitled to any relief. 

REASONS 


ISSUE NO . 1 


The II party is a Rural Bank established as per the Regional 
Rural Banks Act , 1976 . The sponsor bank is the Canara 
Bank . It has branches in the villages of Raichur and Bellary 
districts. The employees of the bank are governed by Tunga 
bhadra Gramin bank (Staff ) Service Regulations, 1980 , Ini 
tially the party was taken as a trainee field supervisor on 
the terms and conditions, as shown in the proceedings dated 
11 - 5 - 1983 . He accepted the said terms and conditions and . . 
joined as a trainee . As per the said proceedings , he was 
appointed as a trainee for an initial period of six months 
and it was liable to be extended , if found necessary . The 
11 party had reserved the right to discontinue his training 
without assigning any reason . During the period of training , 
his performance was not satisfactory. The Branch Manager 
of vojain had given a number os reports in regard to his 
work . His shortcomings were brougnt to his notice and 
he was advised to improve . In order to notivate him and 
give him experience , he was posted to different branches and 
sent to work under different working conditions . He was 
given opportunity to get hiniself trained in different branches . 
However, his performance during the period of training was 
not satisfactory . For that reason , the period of training 
was extended for three months with effect from 25 - 11 -83 . 
Still then , he did not show any improvement nor did he show 
any inclination to learn work . However , on humanitarian 
considerations, it was hoped that he inay improve during the 
period of probation and hence he was appointed as a proba 
tionary supervisory with effect from 26 - 6 - 84 , as per the 
proceedings dated 6 - 7 - 84 . He accepted the terms and condi 
tions of the appointment as per the order dated 6 - 7 - 1984 
and joined as a probationery field supervisor . In accordance 
with the said rules, the period of probation was of two years, 
unless extended as per the service regulations. If the per 
formance was found to be nor satisfactory , the lI party had 
å right to extend the period oi probation . As per clause 
12 of the proceedings dated 6 - 7 -84 , if his performance was 
found to be not satisfactory during the period of probation 
or extended period of probation , the management had the 
right to terminate his services without assigning any reason 
by giving one month s notice or by tendering one 
salary in lieu thereof . . His performance was found to be 
not satisfactory and on several occasions, the BranchManagers 
and other officers had advised him to correct himself 
and show improvement in his performance , but it was of no 
avail. During the period of probation , he remained absent 
and did not show any interest to learn work . He was very 
casual in his work . In order to give him one more oppor 
tunity , the period of probation was extended upto 9 - 4 . 87 by 
· proceedings dated 9 - 6 - 86 . In spite of several opportunities , 
he did not show any improvement. The Il party therefore 
in accordance with the reguiations and the terms of appoint 
ment terminated his service during the period of extended 
probation . The action of the management is bona fide . 
The termination did not attach any stigdia . It is based on 
objective evaluation of his performance . The action of the 
management does not amourt to retrenchment. The various 
allegations made by him are not correct. They are denied , 
His contention that he was a workman under section 2 ( s ) 
of the I. D . Açt is not tenable . As per the said regulations, 
te is an officer, designated as field supervisor, His duties 
and functions were of the supervisory nature . He was not 
a workman . The allegation that his termination is violative 
of Articles 14 , 16 , 19 and 21 is not tenable . Even otherwise , 
the II party has produced sufficient maierial to justify its 
action in not confirming him . The allegation that the action 
is violative of principles of natural justice or Section 2 (00 ) 
or Chapter VB is not correct. The employees of the II 
party bank are expected to be of higher integrity and honesty , 
since the bank deals with the public money . The perfor 
mance of the I party has shaken the confidence of the II 
party reposed in him . He cannot claim reinstatement. The 
reference may be rejected . 


9 . The I party employee , WW - 1 Ishwar D . Audi has 
sworn that the post of the field supervisor is not an Officer s 
post . It has been , however , brought out in his cross- exami 
nation that as per Regulation 3 ( 2 ) , of Ex . M - 12 , a field 
supervisor is included in the definition of an Officer . He 
lias also been cross -examined whether he is a member of 
the Tungabhadra Gramin Bank Officers Association and 
whether the said association is a constituent of the Officers 
Federation . In para 28 of his evidence MW - 1 Shri Vijaya 
Rao , the Senior Manager of the Head Office states that 
field supervisor is an officer and that the nature of his duties 
is supervisory . However , he admits that he has to assess 
the creditworthiness of the parties i. e . borrowers . It is 
admitted by him that the Circular Ex . M -39 is the document 
showing the nature of duties of a field supervisor . The em 
ployee has produced a similar copy at Ex . W - 2 . From Ex . 
M - 39 or Ex , W - 2 it is obvious that the duties and responsi 
bilities of a field supervisor are to visit farms and business 
premises and to assess the loan requirements of the parties , 
to do follow -up inspection and verify whether the land 
has been utilised to assess the intending borrowers to fill - up 
forms etc ., to advise borrowers on technical matters , to 
resort about his work , to co spot inspection , to submit 
monthly report to visit the branch at least once in a day , . 
to do work connected with the field duties to identify prospec 
tive deposition etc . These duties cannot be called as of nature 
of the supervisory duties. 


10 . The learned counsel for the į party cited the case of 
S . K . Verma Vs. Mahesh Chandra and Another ( 1983) 4 SCC 
page 214 . The authority states that appellation of the post 
is not material whereas the court shall have to look into 
The nature of the duties to (ind ou : whether the work is of 
a supervisory nature . - In view of the principle laid down 
in the authority , in the context of the facts placed before 
ins, I find that the fact that whether he is a member of 


3614

-

N 


LARIN 


the Officer s association or not is not the criterion and that 
the nature of his duties, as shown in Ex . M -39 or W - 2 
shall have to be the criterion and that as the said documents , 
ie was a workman within the meaning of the Section 2ís) 
of the I D . Act. . : 


lias power to peer below the order of vermination to find out 
foundation of such an order . It is also stated that if any 
stigma is case on the employee , he is entitled to defend 
himself in proceedings under the rules . It is reiterated that 
the case of the manageineni is that his work was found to be 
unsatisfactory during the period of probation and that no 
stigma was attached . 


ADDITIONAL ISSUES NO . 2 , 3 AND POINT OF REFE 
RENCE 


11. In the counter statement, it has been repeatedly stated 
that his work was not satisfactory , he was informed about it , 
he did not improve nor did he show any inclination to learn 
work and that he was not continued in service , for the reason 
that liis work was not satisfactory during the period of pro 
hatión . - It has been further asserted that the termination of 
his services is without attaching any stigma and it was based 
on objective valuation of his performance and that it does 
not amount to retrenchment and that the provisions of Section 
2 ( od ) did not come into play , since the case came under the 
Clause 2 (00 )( bb ) of the I. D . Act. 

12 . On the other hand , the I party employee has conten 
ded that he has worked delinquently , honestly and sincerely 
and that the termination of his services is illegal. 


18 . The learned counsel for the I party referred to the 
case of Ishwar Chara Jain Vs High Court of Punjab and 
Haryatla and Another ( 1988 ) 3 Supreme Court Cases Page 
370 ). The rule enunciated in the authority is that if the 
contention is of unsatisfactory work and condrict , conclusion 
must be based on existent and relevant material and that it 
shall be subject to judicial review . In the context of the 
facts of the reported casa , it was held that the conclusions 
Were tused on non - existchi and irrelevant material. The facts 
of thc present case would be gone through and theii a finding 
would be recorded in that connection . 


13 . In order to substantiate his said contenticn , the learned 
counsel for the I party cited the case of the Management 
of the Express Newspapers ( Private ) Ltd . , Madurai Vs . The 
Presiding Officer, Labor Court, Madurai (AIR 1964 Sup 
reme Court page 806 ) . From the facts of the reported case , 
it is to be seen that even after the period of probation of 
six months, the employee had been continued in the service 
without any specific order and in that context , it has been 
held that the employee antioned as a probationer even after 
six months. In the case at hand , it has been shown that 
after the initial period of probation , a specific order has been 
passed extending the period of his pro ation . From Regula 
tion 8 ( 1 ) of Ex . M - 12 . it is obvious that every officer on his 
appoint to a post in the bank shall be on probation for a 
period of two years and that it shall be extendable up to a 
period of not extending one year . Since the facts of the 
presert case are differen! .. it cannot be said that the I party 
eniployee had gained any permanent status or that his services 
have been terminated after the period of probation . In my 
View the principle laid down in the authority is , therefore , 
of no assistance to the I party employee 


19. The learned coursel for the Il party mainly relied 
tipon the case of the Managenierit of City of Bangalore 
Municipal Corporation Employees Co - operative Society Ltd . 
V s , E . V . Raju ( 1986 TLR (Kur . , page 2372 ). The Hon ble 
High Court of Karnataka has laid down in the said authority 

lat protection in law is not for inisconduct but it is 
against unlawful termination and that dismissal in Common 
Law with a mouth : notice or a month s wages was possible 
but dismissal under the Industrial Disputes Act entails rein 
statement and payment of back wages , in case the dismissal 
is found to be improper and that every termination feed 
not be preceded by a domestic enquiry . It has been further 
stated that in adjudication of a dispute beilig raised , the 
initial question would be whether there was good rcason 
ter dismissal and the next question would te wlicilier proper 
proseduir has been follow : d and that failure to hold domestic 
enquiry or failure to mani specific charges of niisconduct 
would not take away urisdiction of the labour Court 10 
consider the evidence placed before it . The pleading of the

14 . The learned counsel for the I party has then cited the 
case of Gurdial Singh Vs. State of Punjab ( AIR 1979 Sup 
reme Court page 1622 ). The principle laid down in the 
: authority is that an adverse report in a confidential record 
cannot be acted upon to deny promotional opportunities 
unless it was communicate . t to the person concerned and an 
opportunity was given him to improve his work and con 
duct or to explain the circumstances leading to such a report . 
In the present case , the II party has contended that the I 
party employee had been advised in the matter and the 
compliance or grievances raised against him by the customers 
of the bank or the shortcomings in his work found by the 
clicers have been communicated to him . It was argued 
for the II party that this is not a case wherein any adverse 
confidential report had been taken into account, without his 
being given an opportunity of improving himself . The 
evidence in that connection is still to be discussed . 


20 . The learned counsel for the Il party cited the case 
of thick of India and others Vs . R . S . Dhaba 0969 (3 ) 
Suprem : Court Cases page 603 ) and contended that the test 
for attracting article 311 ( 2 ) of the Constitution is whether 
the misconduct or negligence is a mere motive for the order 
of terrination of errine or whether it is the very foundation 
of order of termination of service . It was further argued 
that in the case at hand his conduct as regards his dealings 
with the customers of the bank or as regards the general 
worker as regards his attendance form only the motive to 
arrive at a conclusion that his work was not satisfactory 
during ihe period of probation 


21 . Reliance was also placed on the authority of Suresh 
Chand Vs . Principal, Government Girls Degree College , 
M . P . Khandwa and another ( 1972 II L .LJ. page 363). 
The authority states that if the termination is merely moti 
vated by the misconduct and is not the fourdaion of the 
order , it cannot be said that the termination is unsustain 
able . 


: . 15 . The case of Rasiklal Vagbajibhai Patel Vs. Ahmedabad 
Municipal Corporation and Another ( 1985) 2 Supreme Court 
Cases page 35 ) was cited to substantiate the contention that 
Unless an act is shown as a misconduct in the standing orders 
or Scivice Regulations. the management cannot proceed 
against an employee alleging that he his committed in act 
of misconduct. 


· 22 . The case of State of Uttar Pradesh Vs . Bhoop Singh 
Verma ( 1979 2 Supreme Court Cases page 111) was also 
brought to my notice to support the contention that if there 
is only an order of simple discharge based on unsuitability 
for retention in service , it is not essential that there should be 
a departmtntal enquiry. 


16 . The learned counser for the JI party . in reply contended 
that it is not a case of punishment imposed upon an employee 
for any act of misconduct, but it is a case of unsatisfaciory 
work during the period of probation . Since the facts of the 
case differ , I am of the view that the authority is or no avail 
to the I party .

AWARD 


42 . The learned counsel for the II party contended that 
the termination before the extended period of probation is 
illegal. The principle laid down in the authorities, as dis 
cussed above , justify the action of the management that it 
can terminate the services of an employee soon after he finds 
that he has not completed and it is not likely that he will 
complete the period of probation satisfactorily . The provi 
sions of Section 2 (00 ) (bb ) justify the said action of the 
management, since the I party employee has executed tho 
agreements , such as , Exs. M - 1 and M - 11, and the action 
of the management cannot be said to bo an act of rotronch 
ment. 


By Order No. L -12012 / 57 /86 -D . IV (A ) dated 21- 8-87 tho 
Government of India , Ministry of Labour referred the 
following dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication :

Express Bank Ltd . ( Travel Division ) , 21 Old Court 
House Street , Calcutta in not allowing Shri Hardco 
Singh , Messongor , to rosuno his duties w . . . f . 25 - 11 -85 
is fair and justified ? If not, to what rellof the work 

man is entiled ? " 
2 . When the case is called out today , Mr. P . K . Mukherjee 
Advocate appears for the management and Ales a Joint Poti 
tion of Compromisc, duly signed by both parties. He pray: 
for an Award in terms of the Joint Petition of Compromiso . 
Considered the sald Joint Petition of Compromiso as well as 
the submission of Mr. Mukherjee . The terms of the Joint 
Petition of Compromise appear to be fair , reasonable 
and in the interest of the parties . I therefore , accept the samo 
and rags ad Award in terms of the said Joint Petition of 
Compromise which do form part of this Award as Anne 
xure A . 


43 . The learned counsel for the II party argued that the 
( Staff ) Service Regulations , En . M - 12 and the reply givea 
him , as per Ex . M - 49 , when read along with the evidence 
of the management establish that he did not complete tho 
period of probation satisfactorily and that the management 
was justified in torminating his services. Specific grounds have 
been shown in Ex . M -38 for terminatlog his services and the 
evidoncc produced by the management justiften the said 
action . 


This is my Award . 


44 . On going through the evidence in between the lipos, 
I find that the I party employee is not entitled to any reljef ,

the management had proposed to close tho matter by inflicting 
a lesser punishment, but that he had not availed of th¢ same 
and therefore further proceedings wero hold and that sina 
he had committed acts of misconduct, the management found 
that it was not in the interest of the bank to keep him in 
their einployment. The contention of the workman that the 
management has discriminated against him , in as much as bo 
has been dismissed from service , whereas the Branch Mana 
ger Krishnamurthy Bhat and another employee Rajanna have 
been retained in service by imposing lesser punishments , can 
not bo accepted . Clause No . 19 . 12 ( 3 ) states that an enquiry 
need not be held if the bank had issued a show - cause notico 
to the employoo , advising him of the misconduct and the 
punishment for which he may be liable for the samo, and 
if , the employee makes voluntary admission of his guilt in 
reply to the show - cause notice . Je the management had cxer 
cised its discretion to proceed under the aforesaid clauso 
against all the three of them viz .. Krishnamurthy Bhat, 
Rajanna and tho I party Sri S . S . Şampat Kumar and if the 
former two have accopted and admitted and if the manage 
ment had imposed on them lesser pudushmonts , it cannot be 
said that the management had indulged in any discriminatory 
act or has indulged in unfair labour practice . 


19 . The learned counsel for the I party strongly contended 
that the punishment imposed on the l party workman is highly 
disproportiopate , besides being discriminatory . He placed te 
liance on the case of R . M . Parmar Vs. Gujarat Electricity 
Board ( 19 I L . L . J. page 261 ). The authority states that an 
employce is not bound to admit the charges or plead guilty in 
order to invoke the jurisdiction of the court, under Section 
11 - A of the I. D . Act and that even if he is in fact guilty , 
he has the right to invoko Section 11 - A of the Act 
for reduction of the penalty . The authority doog 
not support the contention of tho I party that tho 
management has discriminated against the employee . 
Şince the discretion to dispenso with the domestic enquiry 
by pro posing lesser punishment is vested in the management, 
by virtue of the bi-partite settlement as shown above , it has 
been held that the management has not discriminated against 
the I party employee . As regards the jurisdiction and power 
of this court , there can be no two opinions that even if the 
employec does pot admit and even after a prolonged enquiry 
or trial, he has been found guilty , he can crave the indulgence 
of the court by iavoking the provisions of Section 11 - A of the 
Act. 


20 . The learned counsol for the I party then cited the 
case of Scooter India Limited Lucknow Ve . Labour Court , 
Lucknow (AIR 1989 Supreme Court page 149 ) . The principle 
laid down in the authority is that even if the disciplinary co 
quiry is fair and lawful and even if the findings are not 
vitiated , the Labour Court has the power to interfore and 
order for reinstatement otc . and that the approach should be 
reformative .

23 . The learned counsel then cited the case of Wimco 
Sramik Union Vs. Seventh Industrial Tribunal ( 1987 LAB . 
I. C . Page 77 ). The rule enunciated in the authority is that 
the ground that the workman had roodered long service and 
that the stolen property WAS worth of Rs. 150 only did 
not justify interference by the Tribunal. 


24 . There is no doubt that the I party employce had 
committed an act of misconduct which in prejudicial and 
detrimontal to the interests of the bank . The rocord further 
shows that the management has been rightly Ivstified in in 
posing a different punishment on the employee as compared 
to the punishment imposed on Krishnamurthy Bhat, Rajanna 
and William D Souza . Taking into account that the approach 
of this Tribunal should be reformative , that tho fact that the 
[ party omployee has not admitted to his gullt hefore the 
management should not be a ground for not invoking the 
provisions of Section 11- A of the T. D . Act. And that he has 
subsequently felt repentant. "I find that thore should be a 
logger punishment than the diannisgal. The employee has put 
forth several grounds that he has been a disabled ex - servico 
man , that he has an aged mother and unmarried sixtors and 
that he himself is now Aged 35, One cannot forget the fact 
that the management has still retained in It s servico the 
branch manager Krishnamurthy Bhat, who was admittedly 
more regponsible for the said act of misconduct. Merely ho 
cause the party employce was working in the Regional 
Ofice , there was no jurisdiction for the Branch Manager of 
Belagodu hranch to advance loan in the name of fictitious 
perone. Taking into account all these factors , I find that the 
punishment of stoppage of three increments and loss of all 
the backwages would meet the ends of justice .

19 . In I. A . No . 5 , the I party has stated that this Tribunal 
may be pleased to rectify or correct the order dated 
1 - 8 - 1988 . In the affidavit enclosed to the application , it has 
been stated by the I party workman that the Enquiry Officer 
was not an independent person but that he was appointed 
as a Special Officer in Special Grade and he is a retired 
person of the police department . It is further alleged that 
there will be always bjas against workman by such a person 
and that material witnesses were not examined and that 
principles of natural justice were not being followed and 
thus these points may be considered afresh . Section 153 
of the C .P . C . applies to cases where there is some inedvertent 
defect on error in any proceeding in a suit . The condition 
to effect the amendment is that all such amendments should 
be made for the purpose of determining the seal question 
in issue raised by the parties. The order dt. 1 - 8 -88 on 
the preliminary issue has been passed , after recording evi 
dence and hearing the parties. What is the defect or the 
error has not been pointed out. The I party intends that 
the domestic enquiry should be set aside on the ground 
that the Enquiry Officer is a retired Deputy Superinten ent 
of Police and that material witnesses were not evam 
and that principles of natural justice were not followed . All 
these points have been elaborately dealt with in the order 
dated 1 -8 - 1988 .
